\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]

Naturality: For maps
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
,
g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, and
h:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
, if
f\perp g
, then
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)h=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}gh

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]

Dinaturality: For maps
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Y
,
g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, and
k:Y\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
, if
f\perp g
then
kf\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}kg=k\left(fk\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]

Diagonal: For maps
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
,
f^{\prime}:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
, and
g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, if
f\perp f^{\prime}
and
f\sqcup f^{\prime}\perp g
, then
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})\perp(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
and
(f\sqcup f^{\prime})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
.

A
\perp
-itegory
is a pair
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
, consisting of a
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
with all binary
\perp
-joins and a Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
.

We note that
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
is well defined by Lemma
4.3
.(
iv
). The axioms of a Kleene wand are analogues of the axioms of an iteration operator, but expressed in a setting without coproducts. We first observe that if we have strong disjoint joins, then the fourth axiom can be simplified.

Lemma 5.2

For a finitely disjoint
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
,
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is a Kleene wand if and only if
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
satisfies
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
,
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
,
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
, and the following:

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4.a]

Diagonal: For maps
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
,
f^{\prime}:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
, and
g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, if
f\perp f^{\prime}
,
f\perp g
, and
f^{\prime}\perp g
, then
(f\sqcup f^{\prime})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)

Proof:
For the
\Rightarrow
direction, we need only to explain why
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4.a]
holds. So suppose that
f\perp f^{\prime}
,
f\perp g
, and
f^{\prime}\perp g
. By
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.4]
, we get that
f\sqcup f^{\prime}\perp g
. Then we can apply
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
to get that
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})\perp(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
and the rest of the statement of
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4.a]
. For the
\Leftarrow
direction, we need to explain why
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
holds. So suppose that
f\sqcup f^{\prime}\perp g
. We first need to explain why
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})
and
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
are
\perp
-disjoint. To do so, first note that by Lemma
4.3
.(
iv
), we get that
f\perp f^{\prime}
,
f\perp g
, and
f^{\prime}\perp g
. Then observe that by using
[R.1]
and
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
, we can compute that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{g}g)=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{g})g
, so
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{g})g
and similarly
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime}=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f^{\prime}})f^{\prime}
. Now by Lemma
3.5
.(
v
), since
f\perp f^{\prime}
and
f\perp g
, then
f\perp\overline{f^{\prime}}
and
f\perp\overline{g}
. Moreover by
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.4]
, we get that
f\perp\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g}
, so we can consider
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g})
. Now note that by Lemma
3.5
.(
ii
) and Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
), we get that
(\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g})g=g
and
(\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g})f^{\prime}=f^{\prime}
. Therefore using these identities and
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
, we also get that
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g})\right)f^{\prime}=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime}
and
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g})\right)g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. Now since
f^{\prime}\perp g
, by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.4]
, we then get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime}=\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g})\right)f^{\prime}\perp\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{f^{\prime}}\sqcup\overline{g})\right)g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. So we do indeed get that
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})\perp(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. Then we can apply
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4.a]
to get the rest of
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
.

\Box

Intuitively, the Kleene wand
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
“iterates” the endomorphism
f
until the “guard”
g
is satisfied and then produces that output. This intuition is indeed justified by successive applications of the iteration axiom.

Lemma 5.3

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a
\perp
-restriction category, and
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
and
{g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A}
maps such that
f\perp g
. Then for all
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\neq m
,
f^{n}g\perp f^{m}g
(where by convention,
f^{0}=1_{X}
). Furthermore, if
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
is a
\perp
-itegory, then for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the following equality holds:

\displaystyle f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g\sqcup fg\sqcup f^{2}g\sqcup\ldots\sqcup f^{n}g\sqcup f^{n+1}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)

So in particular,
f^{n}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof:
Suppose that
f\perp g
. Then by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.5]
,
\overline{f}\perp\overline{g}
. However, since
\overline{f^{n+1}g}\leq\overline{f}
(using
[R.1]
and
[R.3]
), by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.3]
we then have that
\overline{f^{n+1}g}\perp\overline{g}
. So by Lemma
3.2
,
f^{n+1}g\perp g
. Then by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.4]
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, pre-composing by
f^{k}
gives us that
f^{n+k+1}g\perp f^{k}g
. From here we can conclude that
f^{n}g\perp f^{m}g
for
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\neq m
, as desired.

Now suppose
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
is a
\perp
-itegory. We prove (
5.3
) by induction on
n
. The base case
n=0
is precisely
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
, since
g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. So suppose that we have shown that (
5.3
) for all
0\leq k\leq n
. We now show it for the case
n+1
by showing that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
is the
\perp
-join of the family
\{g,fg,\ldots,f^{n}g,f^{n+1}g,f^{n+2}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\}
. By the induction hypothesis and
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.1]
, we already have that
f^{k}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
for all
0\leq k\leq n
and also that
f^{n+1}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. So we also need to explain why
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
is greater than
f^{n+1}g
and
f^{n+2}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. However using
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
and
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.3]
, observe that
f^{n+1}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)=f^{n+1}g\sqcup f^{n+2}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. So since
f^{n+1}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
, by
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.1]
and transitivity, we get that
f^{n+1}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
and
f^{n+2}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. So
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.1]
holds for
n+1
. Now suppose that we have a map
h
such that
f^{k}g\leq h
for all
0\leq k\leq n+1
and
f^{n+2}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq h
. By
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.2]
, we also get that
f^{n+1}g\sqcup f^{n+2}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq h
, or in other words,
f^{n+1}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq h
. So by the induction hypothesis and
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.2]
, we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq h
. So
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.2]
holds for
n+2
. So we conclude that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
is the
\perp
-join of
\{g,fg,\ldots,f^{n}g,f^{n+1}g,f^{n+2}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\}
. Thus (
5.3
) holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
as desired.

\Box

The above lemma suggests that a natural example of a Kleene wand should be the disjoint join of all
f^{n}g
when it exists. This formula clearly realizes the intuition that
f
is iterated until it falls in the domain of
g
. In fact, in Thm
5.16
, we will show that for a disjoint interference restriction category, this formula always defines a Kleene wand. In the meantime, here are our main examples of Kleene wands:

Example 5.4

\mathsf{PAR}
has a Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
with respect to
\perp_{0}
defined as follows for
\perp_{0}
-disjoint partial functions
{f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X}
and
g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
:

\displaystyle(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)(x):=\begin{cases}g(x)&\text{if $g(x)\downarrow$}\\
g(f^{n+1}(x))&\text{if there exists a (necessarily unique) $n\in\mathbb{N}$ such that $g(f^{n+1}(x))\downarrow$}\\
\uparrow&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Note that this is well-defined since for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
with
m\neq n
, if
g(f^{m}(x))\downarrow
then
g(f^{n}(x))\uparrow
. Thus
(\mathsf{PAR},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
is a
\perp_{0}
-itegory.

Example 5.5

(\mathsf{REC},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
is a
\perp_{r}
-itegory where the Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is defined as in the previous example.

Example 5.6

The category of timed sets as introduced in
[
13
]
is a
\perp_{0}
-itegory. This was the original example for the original definition of a Kleene wand (which recall was only for
\perp_{0}
). We will not review timed sets here and invite the curious reader to see
[
13
]
for more details.

Here are some other useful basic properties about Kleene wands.

Lemma 5.7

In a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
, for maps
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
,
g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, and
g^{\prime}:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, which are pairwise
\perp
-disjoint, we have that:

(i)

f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{g})g

(ii)

0\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g

(iii)

f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}0=0

(iv)

If
f\perp g\sqcup g^{\prime}
, then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\perp f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
and
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(g\sqcup g^{\prime})=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\sqcup f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}

(v)

If
f
is total, then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=0
.

(vi)

If
g
is total, then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g
.

Proof:
These are mostly straightforward to prove.

(i)

This follows from
[R.1]
and
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
, since
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{g}g)=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{g})g
.

(ii)

By Lemma
3.5
.(
i
),
0\perp g
. So using
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
, we have that
0\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g\sqcup 0(0\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)=g\sqcup 0=g
.

(iii)

Using (
i
), we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}0=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{0})0=0
.

(iv)

Suppose that
f\perp g\sqcup g^{\prime}
. By Lemma
3.5
.(
iv
) and Lemma
4.3
.(
ii
), we also get that
f\perp\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}}
. As such, we can take their Kleene wand:
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}})
. Next note that by Lemma
3.5
.(
ii
) and Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
), we get that
(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}})g=g
and
(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}})g^{\prime}=g^{\prime}
. Therefore, by
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
, we get that
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}})\right)g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
and
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}})\right)g^{\prime}=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
. Now since
g\perp g^{\prime}
, by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.4]
we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}})\right)g\perp\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}})\right)g^{\prime}=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
. So we have that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\perp f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
. Finally, using (
i
) and Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
) and (
ii
), we compute that:

\displaystyle f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(g\sqcup g^{\prime})=\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{g\sqcup g^{\prime}}\right)(g\sqcup g^{\prime})=\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\left(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}}\right)\right)(g\sqcup g^{\prime})=\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\left(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}}\right)\right)g\sqcup\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\left(\overline{g}\sqcup\overline{g^{\prime}}\right)\right)g^{\prime}=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\sqcup f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}

So
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(g\sqcup g^{\prime})=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\sqcup f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
as desired.

(v)

If
f
is total, then by Lemma
3.5
.(
iii
),
f\perp g
implies that
g=0
. So by (
iii
),
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}0=0
.

(vi)

If
g
is total, then by Lemma
3.5
.(
iii
),
f\perp g
implies that
f=0
. So by (
ii
),
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=0\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g
.

\Box

We now turn our attention to discussing uniformity and inductivity of Kleene wands. These are analogues of their namesakes for iteration/trace operators, which are often desirable properties of well-behaved iteration/trace operators
[
30
]
.

Definition 5.8

In a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
, the Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is said to be:

(i)

Uniform
if when
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
hg^{\prime}=g
and
hf^{\prime}=fh
, then
h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
;

(ii)

Lax uniform
if when
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
g\leq hg^{\prime}
and
fh\leq hf^{\prime}
, then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
;

(iii)

Colax uniform
if when
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
g\geq hg^{\prime}
and
fh\geq hf^{\prime}
, then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\geq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
.

Basic uniformity simply asks that the iteration behaves equivariently for homomorphic iteration data. The (co)lax versions of uniformity ask that (co)lax homomorphism between iteration data produces a commensurate (co)lax behaviour between iterants. Here are some equivalent alternative descriptions of uniformity.

Lemma 5.9

In a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
,

(i)

\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform if and only if when
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
ag^{\prime}=gb
and
af^{\prime}=fa
, then
a\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)=\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)b
;

(ii)

\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is lax uniform if and only if when
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
gb\leq ag^{\prime}
and
fa\leq af^{\prime}
, then
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)b\leq a\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
;

(iii)

\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is colax uniform if and only if when
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
gb\geq ag^{\prime}
and
fa\geq af^{\prime}
, then
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)b\geq a\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
;

Proof:
For the
\Rightarrow
direction of (
i
), suppose that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform. Now also suppose that
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and also that
ag^{\prime}=gb
and
af^{\prime}=fa
. Now by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.4]
, we have that
f\perp gb
. As such, setting
h=a
, we can apply uniformity to get that
a(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime})=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}gb
. However by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.2]
, we have that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}gb=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)b
. So we get that
a(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime})=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}gb
. Conversely, for the
\Leftarrow
direction of (
i
), suppose that
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and also that
hg^{\prime}=g
and
hf^{\prime}=fh
. Setting
a=h
and
b=1
, we get that
h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
, and so conclude that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform. Thus (
i
) holds as desired. Proving (
ii
) and (
iii
) is done in a similar fashion.

\Box

From the alternative description of (co)lax uniformity, we can show that a (co)lax uniform Kleene wand is monotone.

Corollary 5.10

In a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
, if
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is (co)lax uniform then if
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
g\leq g^{\prime}
and
f\leq f^{\prime}
, then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
.

Proof:
This follows immediately by setting
a=1
and
b=1
in Lemma
5.9
.(
ii
) (resp. (
iii
)).

\Box

Note that being uniform and lax (resp. colax) uniform implies being colax (resp. lax) uniform, and also that being both lax and colax uniform implies being uniform.

Lemma 5.11

In a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
, the following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform and lax uniform;

(ii)

\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform and colax uniform;

(iii)

\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is lax uniform and colax uniform.

Proof:
We show that
(i)\Rightarrow(ii)\Rightarrow(iii)\Rightarrow(i)
. Starting with
(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
, suppose that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform and lax uniform. We need to show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is also colax uniform. So suppose that
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and also that
hg^{\prime}\leq g
and
hf^{\prime}\leq fh
. Then this says that
\overline{hg^{\prime}}g=hg^{\prime}
and
\overline{hf^{\prime}}fh=hf^{\prime}
. Now note that
\overline{hf^{\prime}}f\leq f
and
\overline{hg^{\prime}}g\leq g
, so by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.2]
,
\overline{hf^{\prime}}f\perp\overline{hg^{\prime}}g
. Then by uniformity, we have that
h(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime})=\overline{hf^{\prime}}f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{hg^{\prime}}g
, while by Cor
5.10
, we also have that
\overline{hf^{\prime}}f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{hg^{\prime}}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. So by transitivity we have that
h(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime})\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
, and so we conclude that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is colax.

By a similar argument, we can also that
(ii)\Rightarrow(iii)
, since we would need to show that being uniform and colax uniform implies being lax uniform. So it remains to show that
(iii)\Rightarrow(i)
. So suppose that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is both lax uniform and colax uniform. Now also suppose that
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and also that
hg^{\prime}=g
and
hf^{\prime}=fh
. Of course, this says that
hg^{\prime}\leq g
and
hf^{\prime}\leq fh
, as well as
hg^{\prime}\geq g
and
hf^{\prime}\geq fh
. So by applying lax uniformity we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
, while applying colax uniformity gives us that
h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. So by antisymmetry, we get that
h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
, and conclude that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform. Thus
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is strongly uniform as desired.

\Box

Definition 5.12

In a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
, the Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is said to be
strongly uniform
if it is both uniform and (co)lax uniform (or equivalently lax uniform and colax uniform).

We can now define what it means for a Kleene wand to be (strongly) inductive.

Definition 5.13

In a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
, the Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is said to be:

(i)

Inductive
if when
f\perp g
,
fh\leq h
, and
g\leq h
then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq h

(ii)

Strongly inductive
if it is both strongly uniform and inductive.

A
(strongly) inductive
\perp
-itegory
is an itegory whose Kleene wand is (strongly) inductive.

We first note that an inductive Kleene wand, if one exists, is the minimal one amongst Kleene wands and, moreover, there is at most one inductive Kleene wand.

Lemma 5.14

Let
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
be an inductive Kleene wand for a
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
with all binary
\perp
-joins, then:

(i)

For any other Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}
, we have for all
f\perp g
that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g
.

(ii)

\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is the unique inductive Kleene wand on
\mathbb{X}
.

Proof:
For (
i
), suppose that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is an inductive Kleene wand and
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}
is another Kleene wand. By
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
for
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}
, we have that
f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g)\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g
and
g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g
. So setting
h=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g
, since
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is inductive, we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g
as desired. Now for (
ii
), suppose that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
and
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}
are both inductive Kleene wands. By (
i
) this means that for all
f\perp g
,
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g
but also
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
. So by antisymmetry of
\leq
, we then get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}^{\prime}g
. So we conclude that an inductive Kleene wand, if one exists, is unique.

\Box

We now show that for a strongly inductive Kleene wand, there is an equivalent axiomatization which is much simpler.

Proposition 5.15

For a
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
with all binary
\perp
-joins,
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is a strongly inductive Kleene wand if and only if
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
satisfies the following axioms:

[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]

If
f\perp g
, then
g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
;

[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]

If
f\perp g
,
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and
ag^{\prime}=gb
and
af^{\prime}=fa
, then
a\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)=\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)b
;

[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]

If
f\perp g
,
fh\leq h
, and
g\leq h
then
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq h
.

Proof:
The
\Rightarrow
direction is automatic since:
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
is immediate from
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
, by Lemma
5.9
.(
i
) we get that
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
is an equivalent way of saying
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform, and
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
is precisely the statement that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is inductive.

For the
\Leftarrow
direction, we need to show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
satisfies
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
,
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
,
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
, and
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
:

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
: It suffices to show that
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. By
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
and since composition is monotone, we first get that
f\left(g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\right)\leq f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. So we have
f\left(g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\right)\leq g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
and
g\leq g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. Thus applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
, we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. So by antisymmetry, we obtain that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
.

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
: If
f\perp g
, then starting with
f\perp g
and
f\perp gh
, setting
a=1
and
b=h
, by applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
we get
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}gh=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)h
as desired.

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
: If
f\perp g
, then starting with
hf\perp hg
and
fh\perp g
, setting
a=h
and
b=1
, by applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
we get
h\left(fh\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)=hf\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}hg
as desired.

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
: Suppose that
f\perp g
and
f\sqcup g\perp h
, which by Lemma
4.3
.(
iv
) also implies that
f\perp h
and
g\perp h
. We first show that
(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\leq(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)
. We first observe that using
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
we can compute that:

\displaystyle(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)

\displaystyle=~(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\sqcup(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)

\displaystyle=~h\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\sqcup\left(g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\right)\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)

\displaystyle=~h\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\sqcup g\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)

(Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
))

\displaystyle=~h\sqcup f\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\sqcup(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)\right)\sqcup g\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)

(Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
))

\displaystyle=~h\sqcup f\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)\sqcup g\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)

\displaystyle=~h\sqcup(f\sqcup g)\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)

(Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
))

So we have that
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)=h\sqcup(f\sqcup g)\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)
. This implies both that
{h\leq(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)}
and
(f\sqcup g)\left((f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\right)\leq(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)
. Then applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
, we get that
(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\leq(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)
.

Next we show that
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
as well. So first observe that using
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
again, we can compute that
(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h=h\sqcup f\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)\sqcup g\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)
. This implies both that
h\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
and
f\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
. Thus applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
, we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
. On the other hand, we also have that
g\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
and
f\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
. So applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
here gives us that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
. However, by
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
, we have that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)
. So we then get that
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
. Therefore, bringing this all together, we have that
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\left((f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\right)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
and
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
. Then applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
again, we get
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)\leq(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h
. So by antisymmetry, we conclude that
(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h)
as desired.

So
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is a Kleene wand. We must also show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is inductive and strongly uniform. However,
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
is precisely the statement that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is inductive, while Lemma
5.9
.(
i
) tells us that
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
is an equivalent way of saying
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is uniform. So it remains to show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is lax uniform as well. So suppose that
f\perp g
and
f^{\prime}\perp g^{\prime}
, and also that
g\leq hg^{\prime}
and
fh\leq hf^{\prime}
. By
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
, recall that we have that
g^{\prime}\leq f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
and
f^{\prime}\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)\leq f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}
. Pre-composing by
h
, we then get
hg^{\prime}\leq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
and
hf^{\prime}\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)\leq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
. By transitivity, we have that
g\leq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
and
fh\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)\leq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
. Therefore, applying
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
, we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq h\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)
. So we have that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is also lax uniform. Therefore, we conclude that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is a strongly inductive Kleene wand.

\Box

By the above proposition, we can now easily show that in a setting with all disjoint joins, there is a canonical strongly inductive Kleene wand. So every (countable) disjoint interference restriction category is canonically a strongly inductive itegory.

Theorem 5.16

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a disjoint
\perp
-restriction category. Then
\mathbb{X}
has a strongly inductive Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
defined as follows for
\perp
-disjoint maps
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
and
g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
:

\displaystyle f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\colon=\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}g

where by convention
f^{0}=1_{X}
. Thus ,
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
is a strongly inductive
\perp
-itegory.

Proof:
First note that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is well-defined by Lemma
5.3
. By Prop
5.15
, to show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is a strongly inductive Kleene wand, it suffices to show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
satisfies the three axioms
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
to
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
.

[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
: Using
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.4]
, we easily compute that:

f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}g=g\sqcup\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n+1}g=g\sqcup f\left(\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}g\right)=g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)

So we have that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
, which of course means that
g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\leq f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g
.

[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
: Suppose that
ag^{\prime}=gb
and
af^{\prime}=fa
. Then by Lemma
4.3
.(
ii
), we easily compute that:

\displaystyle a\left(\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{f^{\prime}}^{n}g^{\prime}\right)=\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}a{f^{\prime}}^{n}g^{\prime}=\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}ag^{\prime}=\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}gb=\left(\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}g\right)b

So
a\left(f^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g^{\prime}\right)=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)b
.

[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
: Suppose that
fh\leq h
and
g\leq h
. Note that
fh\leq h
implies that
f^{n}h\leq h
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
as well. Then we see that
f^{n}g\leq f^{n}h\leq h
. So
f^{n}g\leq h
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. So by
[
\boldsymbol{\sqcup}
.2]
,
\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}g\leq h
, which we rewrite as
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\leq h
.

So we conclude that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is a strongly inductive Kleene wand as desired.

\Box

Example 5.17

The Kleene wands given in Ex.
5.4
and
5.5
are strongly inductive Kleene wands, and so
(\mathsf{PAR},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
and
(\mathsf{REC},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
are both strongly inductive itegories.

Moreover, using the construction from Prop
4.10
, we observe that every interference restriction category embeds into a strongly inductive itegory.

Corollary 5.18

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a
\perp
-restriction category. Then
\left(\mathsf{DJ}\left[(\mathbb{X},\perp)\right],\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\right)
is a strongly inductive
\perp
-itegory, where
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is defined as in Thm
5.16
.

In particular, we can in fact embed any restriction with restriction zeroes into a strongly inductive itegory by considering the maximal interference restriction
\perp_{0}
. It is not difficult to see that the induced interference restriction on
\mathsf{DJ}\left[(\mathbb{X},\perp_{0})\right]
will also be the maximal one.

Corollary 5.19

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a restriction category with restriciton zeroes. Then
\left(\mathsf{DJ}\left[(\mathbb{X},\perp_{0})\right],\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\right)
is a strongly inductive
\perp_{0}
-itegory, where
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is defined as in Thm
5.16
.

We conclude this section by observing that the minimal interference relation has a unique but trivial Kleene wand, which is also strongly inductive.

Lemma 5.20

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a restriction category with restriction zeroes. Then
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta})
is a strongly inductive
\perp_{\delta}
-itegory where
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}g=g

Moreover
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}
is the unique Kleene wand for
(\mathbb{X},\perp_{\delta})
.

Proof:
Since by Lemma
4.7
,
\mathbb{X}
is a disjoint
\perp_{d}
-restriction category, we show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}
is a strongly inductive Kleene wand by checking
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
to
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
. However note that
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
and
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
are immediate by definition of
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}
. For
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
, suppose that
f\perp_{\delta}g
which implies that
f=0
or
g=0
. We need to show that
g\sqcup fg\leq g
. So if
f=0
, then we have that
g\sqcup fg=g\sqcup 0g=g\sqcup 0=g
. If
g=0
, then
g\sqcup fg=0\sqcup f0=0\sqcup f0=0=g
. So in either case we have that
g\sqcup fg=g\leq g
, so
[Alt.
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
holds. Thus
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}
is a strongly inductive Kleene wand. Now suppose we have another Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
for
(\mathbb{X},\perp_{\delta})
, and suppose that
f\perp_{\delta}g
, so either
f=0
or
g=0
. If
f=0
, by Lemma
5.7
.(
ii
),
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=0\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}g
. If
g=0
, by Lemma
5.7
.(
iii
),
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}0=0=g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}g
. So we conclude that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}=\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}
, and therefore
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}_{\delta}
is the unique Kleene wand for
\perp_{\delta}
.

\Box

6
Kleene Upper-Stars

In this section, we take a detour to discuss Kleene wands in the
classical
restriction category setting, that is, in the presence of
relative complements
. In such a setting, we will show that Kleene wands are in fact completely determined by how they act on their endomorphism input. Thus, in the classical setting, Kleene wands are equivalent to an operator on endomorphisms, which we call a
Kleene upper-star
.

We begin by discussing relative complements in a disjoint interference restriction category. Relative complements for join restriction categories were introduced in
[
21
, Sec 13]
. Here, we slightly adjust the definition instead for disjoint
\perp
-restriction categories, where in particular, the relative complement is not simply disjoint in the sense of
[
21
, Prop 6.2]
but
\perp
-disjoint.

Definition 6.1

In a
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
with all binary
\perp
-joins, for parallel maps
{f:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B}
and
g:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
such that
f\leq g
, the
\perp
-relative complement
of
f
in
g
(if it exists) is a (necessarily unique) map
g\backslash f:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
such that:

[
\boldsymbol{\backslash}
.1]

g\backslash f\perp f

[
\boldsymbol{\backslash}
.2]

(g\backslash f)\sqcup f=g

A
classical
\perp
-restriction category
is a
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
with all binary
\perp
-joins such that all
\perp
-relative complements exists.

Intuitively,
g\backslash f
is undefined when
f
is defined and is equal to
g
whenever
f
is undefined. Moreover, a
\perp
-relative complement is a relative complement in the sense of
[
21
, Sec 13]
(which we can now call a
\perp_{0}
-relative complement), and so satisfies the same properties, some of which can be found in
[
21
, Lemma 13.14]
and
[
20
, Lemma 4.9]
.

Recall that
\mathcal{O}(A)
is a bounded meet-semilattice, so every restriction idempotent
e:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
is always less than or equal to the identity
1_{A}:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, so
e\leq 1_{A}
. As such, we can consider the relative complement of a restriction idempotent in the identity, which we denote as
e^{c}:=1_{A}\backslash e
. It turns out that having complements of restriction idempotents is sufficient to have all relative complements.

Lemma 6.2

A
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
with all binary
\perp
-joins is classical if and only if for every restriction idempotent
e:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
, there exists a (necessarily unique) restriction idempotent
e^{c}:A\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
such that
e\perp e^{c}
and
e\sqcup e^{c}=1_{A}
.

Proof:
For
\Rightarrow
direction, define
e^{c}:=1_{A}\backslash e
. Then
e\perp e^{c}
and
e\sqcup e^{c}=1_{A}
are precisely
[
\boldsymbol{\backslash}
.1]
and
[
\boldsymbol{\backslash}
.2]
respectively. For the
\Leftarrow
direction, define
g\backslash f:=\overline{f}^{c}g
. Now since
\overline{f}^{c}\perp\overline{f}
, by Lemma
3.5
.(
iv
), we get that
\overline{f}^{c}\perp f
. Then by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.4]
, it follows that
g\backslash f=\overline{f}^{c}g\perp f
. Next, using that
f\leq g
and Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
), we compute that
(g\backslash f)\sqcup f=\overline{f}^{c}g\sqcup f=\overline{f}^{c}g\sqcup\overline{f}g=\left(\overline{f}^{c}\sqcup\overline{f}\right)g=g
. So
(g\backslash f)\sqcup f=g
. Thus we conclude that
g\backslash f
is indeed the
\perp
-relative complement of
f
in
g
.

\Box

Here is now our main example, and also a non-example.

Example 6.3

\mathsf{PAR}
is a classical
\perp_{0}
-restriction category where for partial functions
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Y
and
{g:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Y}
such that
f\leq g
, the
\perp_{0}
-relative complement
g\backslash f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Y
is the partial function defined as follows:

(g\backslash f)(x)=\begin{cases}g(x)&\text{if }f(x)\uparrow\text{ and }g(x)\downarrow\\
\uparrow&\text{if }f(x)\downarrow\text{ or }g(x)\uparrow\end{cases}

In particular, for a subset
U\subseteq X
, the complement of its associated restriction idempotent
e^{c}_{U}:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
is defined as the restriction idempotent associated to the complement of
U
,
e^{c}_{U}=e_{U^{c}}
. Explicitly:

\displaystyle e^{c}_{U}(x)\colon=\begin{cases}x&\text{ if }x\notin U\\
\uparrow&\text{ if }x\in U\end{cases}

Example 6.4

\mathsf{REC}
is not classical since the complement of a recursively enumerable set is not necessarily recursively enumerable. As such, not every restriction idempotent in
\mathsf{REC}
has a
\perp_{r}
-complement.

We now consider Kleene wands in a classical interference category.

Definition 6.5

A
classical
\perp
-itegory
is a
\perp
-itegory
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
where
\mathbb{X}
is a classical
\perp
-restriction category.

Observe that in a classical
\perp
-restriction category,
f\perp g
implies that
\overline{g}\leq\overline{f}^{c}
, and so
\overline{f}^{c}g=g
. Then in a classical itegory, by
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
, we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c})g
. As such,
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is completely characterized by its endomorphism component. Therefore for a classical interference restriction category, to give a Kleene wand amounts to giving an operation on endomorphisms. We call such an operation a Kleene upper-star.

Definition 6.6

For a classical
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
, a
Kleene upper-star

\star
is a family of operators (indexed by objects
X\in\mathbb{X}
):

\displaystyle\star:\mathbb{X}(X,X)\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\mathbb{X}(X,X)

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}[c]f^{\star}:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Xf:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X\end{matrix}

such that the following axioms hold:

[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.1]

\overline{f}^{c}\sqcup ff^{\star}=f^{\star}

[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.2]

(hf)^{\star}h=h(fh)^{\star}\overline{f}^{c}

[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.3]

If
f\perp g
, then
(f\sqcup g)^{\star}=(f^{\star}g)^{\star}f^{\star}

It is worth noting that the axioms of a Kleene upper-star have a similar flavour to the axioms of a
repetition operator

[
37
, Prop 6.11]
, which can be defined on categories enriched over commutative monoids. We now show that every Kleene wand induces a Kleene upper-star.

Proposition 6.7

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
be a classical
\perp
-itegory. Then
\mathbb{X}
has a Kleene upper-star
\star
defined as follows:

\displaystyle f^{\star}:=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}

Proof:
We show that
\star
satisfies the three Kleene upper-star axioms.

[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.1]

Using
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]
we get that
\overline{f}^{c}\sqcup ff^{\star}=\overline{f}^{c}\sqcup f\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}\right)=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}=f^{\star}
.

[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.2]

Note that since
\overline{fh}\leq\overline{f}
, taking their complements swaps the order, so we have that
\overline{f}^{c}\leq\overline{fh}^{c}
, and so
\overline{f}^{c}\overline{fh}^{c}=\overline{f}^{c}
. Now the analogue of
[R.4]
for complements is that
f\overline{g}^{c}=\overline{fg}^{c}f

[
20
, Lemma 4.9.(ix)]
. Therefore, using this as well as
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
and
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]
, we compute that:

\displaystyle(hf)^{\star}h=\left(hf\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{hf}^{c}\right)h=hf\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{hf}^{c}h=hf\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}h\overline{f}^{c}=h\left(fh\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}\right)=h\left(fh\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{fh}^{c}\overline{f}^{c}\right)=h\left(fh\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{fh}^{c}\right)\overline{f}^{c}=h(fh)^{\star}\overline{f}^{c}

[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.3]

Suppose that
f\perp g
. We have the de Morgan law for complements
\overline{f\sqcup g}^{c}=\overline{f}^{c}\overline{g}^{c}

[
20
, Lemma 4.9.(viii)]
. As such, we have that
f\sqcup g\perp\overline{f}^{c}\overline{g}^{c}
, and thus by
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
, we have that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\perp f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}\overline{g}^{c}
. Then using
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
and
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]
, we compute that:

\displaystyle(f\sqcup g)^{\star}

\displaystyle=~(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f\sqcup g}^{c}

(Def.)

\displaystyle=~(f\sqcup g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}\overline{g}^{c}

(de Morgan Law)

\displaystyle=~(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}\overline{g}^{c})

\displaystyle=~(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}g)\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}\overline{g}^{c})

\displaystyle=~(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c})g\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c})\overline{g}^{c}

\displaystyle=~f^{\star}g\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\star}\overline{g}^{c}

(Def.)

\displaystyle=~f^{\star}g\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f^{\star}g}^{c}f^{\star}

(
[R.4]
for complements)

\displaystyle=~\left(f^{\star}g\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f^{\star}g}^{c}\right)f^{\star}

\displaystyle=~(f^{\star}g)^{\star}f^{\star}

(Def.)

So
\star
is a Kleene upper-star.

\Box

Conversely, we now show that every Kleene upper-star induces a Kleene wand.

Proposition 6.8

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a classical
\perp
-restriction category with a Kleene upper-star
\star
. Then
\mathbb{X}
has a Kleene wand
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g:=f^{\star}g

Proof:
We show that
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
satisfies the four Kleene wand axioms.

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.1]

First observe that if
f\perp g
, then
\overline{g}\leq\overline{f}^{c}
which implies that
\overline{f}^{c}g=g
. So using Lemma
4.3
.(
iii
) and
[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.1]
, we get that:

\displaystyle g\sqcup f(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)=g\sqcup ff^{\star}g=\overline{f}^{c}g\sqcup ff^{\star}g=\left(\overline{f}^{c}\sqcup ff^{\star}\right)g=f^{\star}g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]

This is automatic since
\left(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)h=f^{\star}gh=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}gh
.

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.3]

Again since
f\perp g
, we have that
\overline{f}^{c}g=g
. So using
[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.2]
we compute that:

\displaystyle hf\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}hg=(hf)^{\star}hg=h(fh)^{\star}\overline{f}^{c}g=h(fh)^{\star}g=h\left(fh\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g\right)

[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.44444pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.4]

Suppose that
f\perp f^{\prime}
and
f\sqcup f^{\prime}\perp g
. Now by Lemma
4.3
.(
iv
), we have that
f^{\prime}\perp g
, and so by
[
\boldsymbol{\perp}
.4]
, we have that
f^{\star}f^{\prime}\perp f^{\star}g
, or in other words
(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})\perp(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)
. Next, using
[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.3]
we compute that:

\displaystyle(f\sqcup f^{\prime})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\sqcup f^{\prime})^{\star}g=(f^{\star}f^{\prime})^{\star}f^{\star}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})^{\star}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}f^{\prime})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g)

So
\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}
is a Kleene wand as desired.

\Box

Next we show that these constructions are inverses of each other.

Theorem 6.9

For a classical
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
, there is a bijective correspondence between Kleene wands and Kleene upper-stars. Therefore, a classical itegory is precisely a classical interference restriction category with a Kleene upper-star.

Proof:
We need to show that the constructions of Prop
6.7
and Prop
6.8
are inverses of each other, that is, we need to show that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c})g
and
f^{\star}=f^{\star}\overline{f}^{c}
. For the former, as explained above, since
f\perp g
, we have that
\overline{f}^{c}g=g
. Therefore using
[
\boldsymbol{\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-4.0pt\cr$|$\cr}}}}
.2]
we get that
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c})g
. So
f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}g=(f\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}}\overline{f}^{c})g
. For the other identity, applying
[
\boldsymbol{\star}
.2]
with
h=1_{X}
gives us precisely that
f^{\star}=f^{\star}\overline{f}^{c}
. So the constructions from a Kleene wand to a Kleene upper-star and vice-versa are indeed inverses of each others.

\Box

In the setting where we have all disjoint joins, we can apply the above theorem to the canonical Kleene wand from Thm
5.16
to obtain a Kleene upper-star. This Kleene upper-star is similar to the repetition operator in a setting with infinite sums.

Corollary 6.10

A classical
\perp
-restriction category
\mathbb{X}
which is a countable disjoint
\perp
-restriction category, has a Kleene upper-star
\star
defined as follows on an endomorphism
f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X
:

\displaystyle f^{\star}\colon=\bigsqcup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}\overline{f}^{c}

(13)

Proof:
This is precisely the associated Kleene upper-star for the canonical Kleene wand defined in Thm
5.16
.

\Box

Example 6.11

(\mathsf{PAR},\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{
\hfil#\hfil\cr$\star$\cr\kern-3.87498pt\cr$|$\cr}}})
is a classical itegory whose induced Kleene upper-star is defined on a partial function
{f:X\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X}
as:

\displaystyle f^{\star}(x):=\begin{cases}x&\text{if $f(x)\uparrow$}\\
f^{n+1}(x)&\text{if there exists a (necessarily unique) $n\in\mathbb{N}$ such that $f^{n+1}(x)\downarrow$ and $f^{n+2}(x)\uparrow$}\\
\uparrow&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

7
Extensive Restriction Categories

We now turn our attention to extensive restriction categories. The objective of this section is to show that every extensive restriction category has a canonical interference relation induced by decisions and, moreover, that extensive restriction categories have binary disjoint joins. This means we can certainly consider Kleene wands for extensive restriction categories.

The underlying structure of an extensive restriction category is that of a
coCartesian restriction category
, that is, a restriction category with finite
restriction coproducts
. Restriction coproducts are coproducts in the usual sense, with the added assumption that the coproduct injection maps are total. So for a category
\mathbb{X}
with finite coproducts, we denote the initial object as
\mathsf{0}
, the coproduct as
+
, the injection maps as
\iota_{j}:A_{j}\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A_{1}+\ldots+A_{n}
, and write the copairing operation as
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-\\
\vdots\\
-\end{smallmatrix}\right]
, that is, for a family of maps
f_{1}:A_{1}\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
, …,
f_{n}:A_{n}\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
, their copairing
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]:A_{1}+\ldots+A_{n}\allowbreak\mathrel{\mathop{\hbox to11.09988pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
is the unique map such that
\iota_{j}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]=f_{j}
